Hi, Doctor. Thanks for fitting me in. I, uh... I hope I'm not wasting your time with this. It feels a little silly even talking about it, to be honest. I mean, I work construction, I'm not exactly a young guy anymore, so I shouldn't really be worrying about this kind of stuff, right? 

But... well, I'm here because I just can't seem to stop focusing on this... this rough, sunken-looking patch on my face. Every time I look in the mirror, or if I see a picture of myself, it's just right there. It makes one whole side of me look really old and beat-up. My wife and the people around me, they keep saying they barely even notice anything, so maybe I'm just making a bigger deal out of it than I should be? I don't know. I just can't stop seeing it. Does that make sense why I'd come in to talk to someone about that?

Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's completely ruining my life or anything like that. I'm still working hard, still providing. But, um... it does get in the way sometimes, I guess. 

Lately, I've been running a little late getting out the door for jobs in the morning. I just... I get stuck looking at it, trying to see if it looks worse in different lighting. And, uh, I've kind of been dodging the site photos we take when we finish a project. I just don't want that side of my face caught on camera, you know? 

And... I guess I've skipped a couple of family game nights recently, too. I just feel like if I sit under those bright living room lights, everyone's just going to be staring at it, even if they say they aren't. I know that probably sounds ridiculous for a guy my age to care about... is that normal? To let something like this mess with your routine? I just don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything.

Well, um... I guess that's the tricky part, right? I mean, when I look in the mirror, or especially if I catch a glimpse of myself in a photo, it's just... it's so obvious to me. It's right there, making that whole side of my face look so old and just... beat-up.

My wife and the people around me, they keep saying they barely even notice anything, so maybe I'm just making a bigger deal out of it than I should be? I don't know. I just can't stop seeing it. Does that make sense why I'd come in to talk to someone about that?\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about the patch on your face been interfering with your daily activities or work?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's completely ruining my life or anything like that. I'm still working hard, still providing. But, um... it does get in the way sometimes, I guess. \n\nLately, I've been running a little late getting out the door for jobs in the morning. I just... I get stuck looking at it, trying to see if it looks worse in different lighting. And, uh, I've kind of been dodging the site photos we take when we finish a project. I just don't want that side of my face caught on camera, you know? \n\nAnd... I guess I've skipped a couple of family game nights recently, too. I just feel like if I sit under those bright living room lights, everyone's just going to be staring at it, even if they say they aren't. I know that probably sounds ridiculous for a guy my age to care about... is that normal? To let something like this mess with your routine? I just don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything.\n\nDoctor: Is this patch on your face the only thing you're concerned about when you look at yourself?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's just the patch. Really, everything else is fine. I mean, I'm a construction worker, I'm used to getting a little banged up, so I don't expect to look like a movie star or anything. \n\nIt's just... it's just that one spot that bothers me. I try not to let it get to me, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around picking myself apart all day. That's not me. It's just that one specific area that I can't seem to get past. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this patch on your face is something that other people can see, or is it just something you notice yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's the tricky part, right? I mean, when I look in the mirror, or especially if I catch a glimpse of myself in a photo, it's just... it's so obvious to me. It's right there, making that whole side of my face look so old and just... beat-up. \n\nBut then, like I said, my wife and the guys at work, they swear they barely even notice anything. They tell me I'm crazy for worrying about it. So, I don't know... maybe they're just being nice? Or maybe I really am just seeing something that isn't as bad as I think it is. I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill here. Do you think it's possible for me to see it so clearly, but for everyone else to just... miss it? I hope I'm not sounding completely unreasonable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the patch on your face isn't as noticeable as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I guess that's why I'm here talking to you, right? Because I know my wife and the guys at work say they don't see it. And part of me wants to believe them, I really do. \n\nBut... when I look in the mirror, or when I see a picture of myself, I just can't stop seeing it. It's just this rough, sunken-in patch, and it makes that whole side of my face look so old and... beat-up. I know I shouldn't let it get to me, and I try to just brush it off. I don't want to be one of those guys who's obsessed with how he looks. \n\nBut it's just so hard when it feels like it's right there staring back at me, you know? I mean, do you think it's possible that I'm just focusing on it too much? I really hope I don't sound like I'm just complaining over nothing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Well, I... I definitely think it's really there. I mean, I'm not making it up in my head, I promise. It's just... I guess I'm here because I can't stop seeing it, you know? Well, um... I guess it has to be possible, right? Since, like I was saying, my wife and the guys at the site swear they don't really see what I'm seeing. So... maybe it really is just me? I don't know for sure.

Well, um... I'd say it's been a few years now, maybe? I don't want to make it sound like it's taking over my whole life or anything. It's not like I sit around all day staring at it instead of working. I still get up, go to the site, put in my hours... I'm a hard worker, you know?

I know I keep saying this, and I hope it's okay that I keep bringing it up... it's just confusing because my wife and the guys at work keep telling me they barely notice anything at all. So I know it might sound like I'm just obsessing over something small, and I really don't want to come across that way. I'm just trying to figure out why I see it so clearly when they don't. Does that make sense? I'm not crazy for thinking it's actually there, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that the patch on your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not actually there at all? I... I guess I haven't really thought about it exactly like that. I mean, I've always just assumed it was a real, physical thing because I can see it when I look in the mirror, you know? \n\nBut... if you think that's a possibility, I mean, you're the doctor, right? I feel like I see it so clearly. But maybe my eyes really are just playing tricks on me? I don't know... is that something that happens to guys like me? If you think it's possible that only I can see it... then I'm willing to believe that. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not being too confusing. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so you can help me figure this out.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing and worrying about this patch on your face?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I just don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything.\n\nDoctor: Is this patch on your face the only thing you're concerned about when you look at yourself?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's just the patch. Really, everything else is fine. I mean, I'm a construction worker, I'm used to getting a little banged up, so I don't expect to look like a movie star or anything. \n\nIt's just... it's just that one spot that bothers me. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this patch on your face is something that other people can see, or is it just something you notice yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I don't want to be one of those guys who's obsessed with how he looks. \n\nBut it's just so hard when it feels like it's right there staring back at me, you know? I definitely think it's really there. I mean, I'm not making it up in my head, I promise. It's just... I guess I'm here because I can't stop seeing it, you know? It's this rough, sunken-looking patch, and whenever I look in the mirror or see a photo, it just jumps out at me. It makes that whole side of my face look so old and beat-up. \n\nBut... ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I mean... yeah, I suppose that's possible. If you're saying that's something that happens, I can definitely see that. 

It's just... it's a little confusing, I guess? Because when I look in the mirror, it just feels so obvious to me. But I know I don't have a medical degree, and I know the mind can do weird things sometimes. I hope I'm not sounding completely unreasonable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the patch on your face isn't as noticeable as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I guess that's why I'm here talking to you, right? Because I know my wife and the guys at work say they don't see it. It's this rough, sunken-looking patch, and whenever I look in the mirror or see a photo, it just jumps out at me. It makes that whole side of my face look so old and beat-up. \n\nBut... I know I keep saying this, and I hope it's okay that I keep bringing it up... it's just confusing because my wife and the guys at work keep telling me they barely notice anything at all. So I know it might sound like I'm just obsessing over something small, and I really don't want to come across that way. I'm just trying to figure out why I see it so clearly when they don't. Does that make sense? I'm not crazy for thinking it's actually there, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that the patch on your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not actually there at all? I... I guess I haven't really thought about it exactly like that. I mean, I've always just assumed it was a real, physical thing because I can see it when I look in the mirror, you know? \n\nBut... if you think that's a possibility, I mean, you're the doctor, right? I'd listen to you. It's just a little hard to wrap my head around it being completely made up. I really don't want to sound like I'm losing my mind or being difficult about this. I just... I feel like I see it so clearly. But maybe my eyes really are just playing tricks on me? I don't know... is that something that happens to guys like me? I hope I'm answering your questions okay, I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure this out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other concerns like this in the past that turned out to be unfounded?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think so. I mean, I've always been a pretty level-headed guy, you know? Just go to work, do my job, come home. I've never been one to... imagine things, or get worked up over nothing. \n\nI mean, maybe I've worried about a weird mole or something before, but... nothing like this. Nothing that just stayed with me like this patch does. I really try not to be a hypochondriac or anything. I hope I don't sound like one. I just... this is the only time I've ever felt this stuck on something about my face. Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face is something that only you can see, and it's not actually visible to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it has to be possible, right? Since, like I was saying, my wife and the guys at the site swear they don't really see what I'm seeing. So... maybe it really is just me? I don't know for sure. \n\nIt's just really hard to wrap my head around that, honestly. I look in the mirror and it just seems so obvious to me. But I really don't want you to think I'm completely losing my marbles or, you know, being stubborn about it. I'm trying to keep an open mind, because you're the expert here, not me. If you think it's possible that only I can see it... then I'm willing to believe that. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not being too confusing. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so you can help me figure this out.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing and worrying about this patch on your face?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'd say it's been a few years now, maybe? I don't want to make it sound like it's taking over my whole life or anything. It's not like I sit around all day staring at it instead of working. I still get up, go to the site, put in my hours... I'm a hard worker, you know? \n\nBut, yeah, I guess it's been a couple of years that I've been... keeping an eye on it. Just checking it when I shave, mostly. It's not a huge deal, really. I just noticed it one day and it sort of stuck with me. I hope that's okay? I mean, I hope it doesn't sound too crazy to have been worrying about it for that long. Is that a normal amount of time for something like this?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of the patch on your face is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I suppose so. If you're saying that's a possibility, then... sure, I could see that. \n\nIt's just... it's tough, you know? Because to me, it feels very real. I'm a pretty grounded guy, usually. \n\nSo if you think my perception is off, I'm... I'm definitely open to hearing that. I'm trying to be cooperative here. Does that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I just want to make sure I'm on the same page as you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still see the patch on your face if multiple doctors examined you and told you it wasn't there?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I know that probably sounds ridiculous for a guy my age to care about... is that normal? To let something like this mess with your routine? I just don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything.\n\nDoctor: Is this patch on your face the only thing you're concerned about when you look at yourself?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's just the patch. Really, everything else is fine. I mean, I'm a construction worker, I'm used to getting a little banged up, so I don't expect to look like a movie star or anything. \n\nIt's just... it's just that one spot that bothers me. I try not to let it get to me, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around picking myself apart all day. That's not me. It's just that one specific area that I can't seem to get past. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this patch on your face is something that other people can see, or is it just something you notice yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's the tricky part, right? I mean, when I look in the mirror, or especially if I catch a glimpse of myself in a photo, it's just... it's so obvious to me. It's right there, making that whole side of my face look so old and just... beat-up. \n\nBut then, like I said, my wife and the guys at work, they swear they barely even notice anything. They tell me I'm crazy for worrying about it. So, I don't know... maybe they're just being nice? Or maybe I really am just seeing something that isn't as bad as I think it is. I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill here. Do you think it's possible for me to see it so clearly, but for everyone else to just... miss it? I hope I'm not sounding completely unreasonable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the patch on your face isn't as noticeable as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I guess that's why I'm here talking to you, right? Because I know my wife and the guys at work say they don't see it. And part of me wants to believe them, I really do. \n\nBut... when I look in the mirror, or when I see a picture of myself, I just can't stop seeing it. It's just this rough, sunken-in patch, and it makes that whole side of my face look so old and... beat-up. I know I shouldn't let it get to me, and I try to just brush it off. I don't want to be one of those guys who's obsessed with how he looks. \n\nBut it's just so hard when it feels like it's right there staring back at me, you know? I mean, do you think it's possible that I'm just focusing on it too much? I really hope I don't sound like I'm just complaining over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that's actually there, or could it be something you're just perceiving?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I definitely think it's really there. I mean, I'm not making it up in my head, I promise. It's just... I guess I'm here because I can't stop seeing it, you know? It's this rough, sunken-looking patch, and whenever I look in the mirror or see a photo, it just jumps out at me. It makes that whole side of my face look so old and beat-up. \n\nBut... I know I keep saying this, and I hope it's okay that I keep bringing it up... it's just confusing because my wife and the guys at work keep telling me they barely notice anything at all. So I know it might sound like I'm just obsessing over something small, and I really don't want to come across that way. I'm just trying to figure out why I see it so clearly when they don't. Does that make sense? I'm not crazy for thinking it's actually there, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that the patch on your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not actually there at all? I... I guess I haven't really thought about it exactly like that. I mean, I've always just assumed it was a real, physical thing because I can see it when I look in the mirror, you know? \n\nBut... if you think that's a possibility, I mean, you're the doctor, right? I'd listen to you. It's just a little hard to wrap my head around it being completely made up. I really don't want to sound like I'm losing my mind or being difficult about this. I just... I feel like I see it so clearly. But maybe my eyes really are just playing tricks on me? I don't know... is that something that happens to guys like me? I hope I'm answering your questions okay, I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure this out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other concerns like this in the past that turned out to be unfounded?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think so. I mean, I've always been a pretty level-headed guy, you know? Just go to work, do my job, come home. I've never been one to... imagine things, or get worked up over nothing. \n\nI mean, maybe I've worried about a weird mole or something before, but... nothing like this. Nothing that just stayed with me like this patch does. I really try not to be a hypochondriac or anything. I hope I don't sound like one. I just... this is the only time I've ever felt this stuck on something about my face. Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face is something that only you can see, and it's not actually visible to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it has to be possible, right? Since, like I was saying, my wife and the guys at the site swear they don't really see what I'm seeing. So... maybe it really is just me? I don't know for sure. \n\nIt's just really hard to wrap my head around that, honestly. I look in the mirror and it just seems so obvious to me. But I really don't want you to think I'm completely losing my marbles or, you know, being stubborn about it. I'm trying to keep an open mind, because you're the expert here, not me. If you think it's possible that only I can see it... then I'm willing to believe that. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not being too confusing. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so you can help me figure this out.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing and worrying about this patch on your face?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'd say it's been a few years now, maybe? I don't want to make it sound like it's taking over my whole life or anything. It's not like I sit around all day staring at it instead of working. I still get up, go to the site, put in my hours... I'm a hard worker, you know? \n\nBut, yeah, I guess it's been a couple of years that I've been... keeping an eye on it. Just checking it when I shave, mostly. It's not a huge deal, really. I just noticed it one day and it sort of stuck with me. I hope that's okay? I mean, I hope it doesn't sound too crazy to have been worrying about it for that long. Is that a normal amount of time for something like this?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of the patch on your face is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I suppose so. If you're saying that's a possibility, then... sure, I could see that. \n\nIt's just... it's tough, you know? Because to me, it feels very real. But I know I'm not a doctor, and I know sometimes the mind can play tricks on people. I really don't want to come across as someone who's completely out of touch with reality. I'm a pretty grounded guy, usually. \n\nSo if you think my perception is off, I'm... I'm definitely open to hearing that. I'm trying to be cooperative here. Does that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I just want to make sure I'm on the same page as you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still see the patch on your face if multiple doctors examined you and told you it wasn't there?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess if a bunch of doctors all looked at it and said there was nothing there... I'd really try to believe them. I mean, you guys are the professionals, right? I wouldn't want to be the guy arguing with a whole room full of experts. \n\nBut... honestly? I think I might still see it when I looked in the mirror. I don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm ignoring medical advice, because I'm really not! It's just... it's so clear to me when I look at my reflection. \n\nBut I'd definitely trust the doctors over my own eyes, you know? If they said it was fine, I'd tell myself it was fine. I hope that's the right answer? I just don't want you to think I'm completely unreasonable or anything. I'm really trying to work with you on this.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social interactions or events specifically because of your concern about the patch on your face?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I completely hide away or anything like that. I really try to be a reliable guy, you know? I still go to work. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest with you—it has gotten in the way a little bit. \n\nSometimes I end up being a little late getting out the door for my construction jobs. Just because I'm... well, I'm checking the mirror, trying to make sure it doesn't look too bad before I leave. I always work hard once I'm there, though! I really don't want you to think I'm a bad employee or anything. And, um, when they take crew photos at the site, I've started sort of dodging those. I just step out of frame or find an excuse to be in the truck. I just don't want it on camera.\n\nAnd... as for social things, I have skipped a few of our family game nights lately. I usually just tell them I'm beat from hauling materials all day, but really, it's just because I don't want everyone sitting across the table looking right at my face. \n\nI feel pretty guilty about it, honestly. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible, skipping out on my family over this. Do you think that's... I mean, is it somewhat normal to feel that way? I'm just trying to handle it the best I can without bothering anyone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face could be something that you're imagining, even if it feels real to you?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I suppose that's possible. If you're saying that's something that happens, I can definitely see that. \n\nIt's just... it's a little confusing, I guess? Because when I look in the mirror, it just feels so obvious to me. But I know I don't have a medical degree, and I know the mind can do weird things sometimes. I really don't want to come across as someone who's completely out of touch with reality or, you know, crazy. I'm usually a pretty level-headed guy, I promise.\n\nSo if you think it's possible that I'm imagining it, or making it out to be worse than it is... I'm definitely open to hearing that. I'm trying to be cooperative here and listen to your expertise. Does that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to consider that your concern about the patch on your face might be related to your thoughts or feelings rather than an actual physical issue?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I'd definitely be willing to consider that. I mean, you're the doctor, and if you think my thoughts or feelings are getting tangled up in this, I'm more than happy to look into that. \n\nI really don't want to be one of those guys who just refuses to listen, you know? I want to get this sorted out, whatever it takes. If it's something in my head making me focus on it so much, I can accept that. I just... I really hope it doesn't mean I'm losing my grip on things. I've always prided myself on being a steady, hardworking guy. \n\nBut whatever you think is the best way to handle this, I'm on board. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful and giving you what you need to figure this out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still believe the patch is there even if I told you, as a doctor, that there is nothing physically wrong with your face?\n\nPatient: Oh, man... um. That's a really good question. I... I honestly don't know for sure. I mean, if you, as a medical professional, looked right at me and said there's absolutely nothing wrong... I would really, really want to believe you. You're the expert, right? I've always respected doctors, and I definitely don't want to be one of those guys who thinks he knows better than the professionals. \n\nBut... I guess if I'm being completely straight with you, I think it might still be a little hard for me to just turn off that feeling? Like, I might still go home and... well, double-check the bathroom mirror, just to see. I don't know. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds incredibly stubborn, and I really don't mean to be difficult. I just see it so clearly when I look. But I would try my absolute best to trust you, I promise. \n\nIs it... is it okay that I'm not totally sure how I'd react? I just want to give you an honest answer without sounding like I'm questioning your judgment.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

My wife and the people around me, they keep saying they barely even notice anything, so maybe I'm just making a bigger deal out of it than I should be? I don't know. I just can't stop seeing it. Does that make sense why I'd come in to talk to someone about that?\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about the patch on your face been interfering with your daily activities or work?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I wouldn't say it's completely ruining my life or anything like that. I'm still working hard, still providing. But, um... it does get in the way sometimes, I guess. \n\nLately, I've been running a little late getting out the door for jobs in the morning. I just... I get stuck looking at it, trying to see if it looks worse in different lighting. And, uh, I've kind of been dodging the site photos we take when we finish a project. I just don't want that side of my face caught on camera, you know? \n\nAnd... I guess I've skipped a couple of family game nights recently, too. I just feel like if I sit under those bright living room lights, everyone's just going to be staring at it, even if they say they aren't. I know that probably sounds ridiculous for a guy my age to care about... is that normal? To let something like this mess with your routine? I just don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything.\n\nDoctor: Is this patch on your face the only thing you're concerned about when you look at yourself?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's just the patch. Really, everything else is fine. I mean, I'm a construction worker, I'm used to getting a little banged up, so I don't expect to look like a movie star or anything. \n\nIt's just... it's just that one spot that bothers me. I try not to let it get to me, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around picking myself apart all day. That's not me. It's just that one specific area that I can't seem to get past. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this patch on your face is something that other people can see, or is it just something you notice yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's the tricky part, right? I mean, when I look in the mirror, or especially if I catch a glimpse of myself in a photo, it's just... it's so obvious to me. It's right there, making that whole side of my face look so old and just... beat-up. \n\nBut then, like I said, my wife and the guys at work, they swear they barely even notice anything. They tell me I'm crazy for worrying about it. So, I don't know... maybe they're just being nice? Or maybe I really am just seeing something that isn't as bad as I think it is. I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill here. Do you think it's possible for me to see it so clearly, but for everyone else to just... miss it? I hope I'm not sounding completely unreasonable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the patch on your face isn't as noticeable as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I guess that's why I'm here talking to you, right? Because I know my wife and the guys at work say they don't see it. And part of me wants to believe them, I really do. \n\nBut... when I look in the mirror, or when I see a picture of myself, I just can't stop seeing it. It's just this rough, sunken-in patch, and it makes that whole side of my face look so old and... beat-up. I know I shouldn't let it get to me, and I try to just brush it off. I don't want to be one of those guys who's obsessed with how he looks. \n\nBut it's just so hard when it feels like it's right there staring back at me, you know? I mean, do you think it's possible that I'm just focusing on it too much? I really hope I don't sound like I'm just complaining over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that's actually there, or could it be something you're just perceiving?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I definitely think it's really there. I mean, I'm not making it up in my head, I promise. It's just... I guess I'm here because I can't stop seeing it, you know? It's this rough, sunken-looking patch, and whenever I look in the mirror or see a photo, it just jumps out at me. It makes that whole side of my face look so old and beat-up. \n\nBut... I know I keep saying this, and I hope it's okay that I keep bringing it up... it's just confusing because my wife and the guys at work keep telling me they barely notice anything at all. So I know it might sound like I'm just obsessing over something small, and I really don't want to come across that way. I'm just trying to figure out why I see it so clearly when they don't. Does that make sense? I'm not crazy for thinking it's actually there, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that the patch on your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not actually there at all? I... I guess I haven't really thought about it exactly like that. I mean, I've always just assumed it was a real, physical thing because I can see it when I look in the mirror, you know? \n\nBut... if you think that's a possibility, I mean, you're the doctor, right? I'd listen to you. It's just a little hard to wrap my head around it being completely made up. I really don't want to sound like I'm losing my mind or being difficult about this. I just... I feel like I see it so clearly. But maybe my eyes really are just playing tricks on me? I don't know... is that something that happens to guys like me? I hope I'm answering your questions okay, I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure this out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other concerns like this in the past that turned out to be unfounded?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think so. I mean, I've always been a pretty level-headed guy, you know? Just go to work, do my job, come home. I've never been one to... imagine things, or get worked up over nothing. \n\nI mean, maybe I've worried about a weird mole or something before, but... nothing like this. Nothing that just stayed with me like this patch does. I really try not to be a hypochondriac or anything. I hope I don't sound like one. I just... this is the only time I've ever felt this stuck on something about my face. Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face is something that only you can see, and it's not actually visible to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it has to be possible, right? Since, like I was saying, my wife and the guys at the site swear they don't really see what I'm seeing. So... maybe it really is just me? I don't know for sure. \n\nIt's just really hard to wrap my head around that, honestly. I look in the mirror and it just seems so obvious to me. But I really don't want you to think I'm completely losing my marbles or, you know, being stubborn about it. I'm trying to keep an open mind, because you're the expert here, not me. If you think it's possible that only I can see it... then I'm willing to believe that. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not being too confusing. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so you can help me figure this out.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing and worrying about this patch on your face?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'd say it's been a few years now, maybe? I don't want to make it sound like it's taking over my whole life or anything. It's not like I sit around all day staring at it instead of working. I still get up, go to the site, put in my hours... I'm a hard worker, you know? \n\nBut, yeah, I guess it's been a couple of years that I've been... keeping an eye on it. Just checking it when I shave, mostly. It's not a huge deal, really. I just noticed it one day and it sort of stuck with me. I hope that's okay? I mean, I hope it doesn't sound too crazy to have been worrying about it for that long. I'm a pretty grounded guy, usually. \n\nSo if you think my perception is off, I'm... I'm definitely open to hearing that. I'm trying to be cooperative here. Does that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I just want to make sure I'm on the same page as you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still see the patch on your face if multiple doctors examined you and told you it wasn't there?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't want to be the guy arguing with a whole room full of experts. \n\nBut... honestly? I think I might still see it when I looked in the mirror. I don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm ignoring medical advice, because I'm really not! It's just... it's so clear to me when I look at my reflection. \n\nBut I'd definitely trust the doctors over my own eyes, you know? If they said it was fine, I'd tell myself it was fine. I hope that's the right answer? I just don't want you to think I'm completely unreasonable or anything. I get stuck looking at it, trying to see if it looks worse in different lighting. And, uh, I've kind of been dodging the site photos we take when we finish a project. I just don't want that side of my face caught on camera, you know? \n\nAnd... I guess I've skipped a couple of family game nights recently, too. I just feel like if I sit under those bright living room lights, everyone's just going to be staring at it, even if they say they aren't. I know that probably sounds ridiculous for a guy my age to care about... is that normal? To let something like this mess with your routine? I just don't want you to think I'm slacking off or anything.\n\nDoctor: Is this patch on your face the only thing you're concerned about when you look at yourself?\n\nPatient: No, no, it's just the patch. Really, everything else is fine. I mean, I'm a construction worker, I'm used to getting a little banged up, so I don't expect to look like a movie star or anything. \n\nIt's just... it's just that one spot that bothers me. I try not to let it get to me, I really do. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around picking myself apart all day. That's not me. It's just that one specific area that I can't seem to get past. Does that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think this patch on your face is something that other people can see, or is it just something you notice yourself?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess that's the tricky part, right? I mean, when I look in the mirror, or especially if I catch a glimpse of myself in a photo, it's just... it's so obvious to me. It's right there, making that whole side of my face look so old and just... beat-up. \n\nBut then, like I said, my wife and the guys at work, they swear they barely even notice anything. They tell me I'm crazy for worrying about it. So, I don't know... maybe they're just being nice? Or maybe I really am just seeing something that isn't as bad as I think it is. I really don't want to sound like I'm making a mountain out of a molehill here. Do you think it's possible for me to see it so clearly, but for everyone else to just... miss it? I hope I'm not sounding completely unreasonable.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any possibility that the patch on your face isn't as noticeable as you think it is?\n\nPatient: Well, I mean... I guess that's why I'm here talking to you, right? Because I know my wife and the guys at work say they don't see it. And part of me wants to believe them, I really do. \n\nBut... when I look in the mirror, or when I see a picture of myself, I just can't stop seeing it. It's just this rough, sunken-in patch, and it makes that whole side of my face look so old and... beat-up. I know I shouldn't let it get to me, and I try to just brush it off. I don't want to be one of those guys who's obsessed with how he looks. \n\nBut it's just so hard when it feels like it's right there staring back at me, you know? I mean, do you think it's possible that I'm just focusing on it too much? I really hope I don't sound like I'm just complaining over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face is something that's actually there, or could it be something you're just perceiving?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I definitely think it's really there. I mean, I'm not making it up in my head, I promise. It's just... I guess I'm here because I can't stop seeing it, you know? It's this rough, sunken-looking patch, and whenever I look in the mirror or see a photo, it just jumps out at me. It makes that whole side of my face look so old and beat-up. \n\nBut... I know I keep saying this, and I hope it's okay that I keep bringing it up... it's just confusing because my wife and the guys at work keep telling me they barely notice anything at all. So I know it might sound like I'm just obsessing over something small, and I really don't want to come across that way. I'm just trying to figure out why I see it so clearly when they don't. Does that make sense? I'm not crazy for thinking it's actually there, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered the possibility that the patch on your face isn't actually there?\n\nPatient: Well, um... not actually there at all? I... I guess I haven't really thought about it exactly like that. I mean, I've always just assumed it was a real, physical thing because I can see it when I look in the mirror, you know? \n\nBut... if you think that's a possibility, I mean, you're the doctor, right? I'd listen to you. It's just a little hard to wrap my head around it being completely made up. I really don't want to sound like I'm losing my mind or being difficult about this. I just... I feel like I see it so clearly. But maybe my eyes really are just playing tricks on me? I don't know... is that something that happens to guys like me? I hope I'm answering your questions okay, I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure this out.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had any other concerns like this in the past that turned out to be unfounded?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I don't think so. I mean, I've always been a pretty level-headed guy, you know? Just go to work, do my job, come home. I've never been one to... imagine things, or get worked up over nothing. \n\nI mean, maybe I've worried about a weird mole or something before, but... nothing like this. Nothing that just stayed with me like this patch does. I really try not to be a hypochondriac or anything. I hope I don't sound like one. I just... this is the only time I've ever felt this stuck on something about my face. Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the patch on your face is something that only you can see, and it's not actually visible to others?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess it has to be possible, right? Since, like I was saying, my wife and the guys at the site swear they don't really see what I'm seeing. So... maybe it really is just me? I don't know for sure. \n\nIt's just really hard to wrap my head around that, honestly. I look in the mirror and it just seems so obvious to me. But I really don't want you to think I'm completely losing my marbles or, you know, being stubborn about it. I'm trying to keep an open mind, because you're the expert here, not me. If you think it's possible that only I can see it... then I'm willing to believe that. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not being too confusing. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers so you can help me figure this out.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been noticing and worrying about this patch on your face?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I'd say it's been a few years now, maybe? I don't want to make it sound like it's taking over my whole life or anything. It's not like I sit around all day staring at it instead of working. I still get up, go to the site, put in my hours... I'm a hard worker, you know? \n\nBut, yeah, I guess it's been a couple of years that I've been... keeping an eye on it. Just checking it when I shave, mostly. It's not a huge deal, really. I just noticed it one day and it sort of stuck with me. I hope that's okay? I mean, I hope it doesn't sound too crazy to have been worrying about it for that long. Is that a normal amount of time for something like this?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that your perception of the patch on your face is not based on reality?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I suppose so. If you're saying that's a possibility, then... sure, I could see that. \n\nIt's just... it's tough, you know? Because to me, it feels very real. But I know I'm not a doctor, and I know sometimes the mind can play tricks on people. I really don't want to come across as someone who's completely out of touch with reality. I'm a pretty grounded guy, usually. \n\nSo if you think my perception is off, I'm... I'm definitely open to hearing that. I'm trying to be cooperative here. Does that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I just want to make sure I'm on the same page as you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still see the patch on your face if multiple doctors examined you and told you it wasn't there?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I guess if a bunch of doctors all looked at it and said there was nothing there... I'd really try to believe them. I mean, you guys are the professionals, right? I wouldn't want to be the guy arguing with a whole room full of experts. \n\nBut... honestly? I think I might still see it when I looked in the mirror. I don't want to sound stubborn or like I'm ignoring medical advice, because I'm really not! It's just... it's so clear to me when I look at my reflection. \n\nBut I'd definitely trust the doctors over my own eyes, you know? If they said it was fine, I'd tell myself it was fine. I hope that's the right answer? I just don't want you to think I'm completely unreasonable or anything. I'm really trying to work with you on this.\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social interactions or events specifically because of your concern about the patch on your face?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I completely hide away or anything like that. I really try to be a reliable guy, you know? I still go to work. But... I guess, if I'm being completely honest—and I want to be honest with you—it has gotten in the way a little bit. \n\nSometimes I end up being a little late getting out the door for my construction jobs. Just because I'm... well, I'm checking the mirror, trying to make sure it doesn't look too bad before I leave. I always work hard once I'm there, though! I really don't want you to think I'm a bad employee or anything. And, um, when they take crew photos at the site, I've started sort of dodging those. I just step out of frame or find an excuse to be in the truck. I just don't want it on camera.\n\nAnd... as for social things, I have skipped a few of our family game nights lately. I usually just tell them I'm beat from hauling materials all day, but really, it's just because I don't want everyone sitting across the table looking right at my face. \n\nI feel pretty guilty about it, honestly. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible, skipping out on my family over this. Do you think that's... I mean, is it somewhat normal to feel that way? I'm just trying to handle it the best I can without bothering anyone.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the patch on your face could be something that you're imagining, even if it feels real to you?\n\nPatient: I mean... yeah, I suppose that's possible. If you're saying that's something that happens, I can definitely see that. \n\nIt's just... it's a little confusing, I guess? Because when I look in the mirror, it just feels so obvious to me. But I know I don't have a medical degree, and I know the mind can do weird things sometimes. I really don't want to come across as someone who's completely out of touch with reality or, you know, crazy. I'm usually a pretty level-headed guy, I promise.\n\nSo if you think it's possible that I'm imagining it, or making it out to be worse than it is... I'm definitely open to hearing that. I'm trying to be cooperative here and listen to your expertise. Does that sound like a reasonable way to look at it? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to consider that your concern about the patch on your face might be related to your thoughts or feelings rather than an actual physical issue?\n\nPatient: Well, um... yeah, I'd definitely be willing to consider that. I mean, you're the doctor, and if you think my thoughts or feelings are getting tangled up in this, I'm more than happy to look into that. \n\nI really don't want to be one of those guys who just refuses to listen, you know? I want to get this sorted out, whatever it takes. If it's something in my head making me focus on it so much, I can accept that. I just... I really hope it doesn't mean I'm losing my grip on things. I've always prided myself on being a steady, hardworking guy. \n\nBut whatever you think is the best way to handle this, I'm on board. Does that make sense? I just want to make sure I'm being helpful and giving you what you need to figure this out.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still believe the patch is there even if I told you, as a doctor, that there is nothing physically wrong with your face?\n\nPatient: Oh, man... um. That's a really good question. I... I honestly don't know for sure. I mean, if you, as a medical professional, looked right at me and said there's absolutely nothing wrong... I would really, really want to believe you. You're the expert, right? I've always respected doctors, and I definitely don't want to be one of those guys who thinks he knows better than the professionals. \n\nBut... I guess if I'm being completely straight with you, I think it might still be a little hard for me to just turn off that feeling? Like, I might still go home and... well, double-check the bathroom mirror, just to see. I don't know. I'm sorry, I know that probably sounds incredibly stubborn, and I really don't mean to be difficult. I just see it so clearly when I look. But I would try my absolute best to trust you, I promise. \n\nIs it... is it okay that I'm not totally sure how I'd react? I just want to give you an honest answer without sounding like I'm questioning your judgment.\n\nDoctor: Would you be able to accept it if I told you that there is no physical evidence of the patch on your face?\n\nPatient: I mean... yes, absolutely. If you did an exam and told me there's no physical evidence, I would accept that. I really would. I know you've got the training and the tools to see things I can't, and I'd never want to sit here and argue with you about your own job. \n\nI guess I'd just... I'd probably have to work on wrapping my head around it, you know? Because it feels so real to me. But if you say it's not there, then I'm going to take your word for it. I really am trying to be a good patient here, and I want to do whatever you think is best. \n\nDoes that... does that sound like the right way to approach this? I just want to make sure I'm not making things harder for you.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's anything that could change your mind about the patch being on your face?\n\nPatient: I... gosh, I don't really know for sure. I mean, I'd like to think so. If you or another doctor showed me some kind of scan or... or maybe if there was some sort of treatment we did and I felt like it was working? I guess I'd want to believe that my mind could be changed. \n\nI really don't want to be stuck like this, you know? I want to be reasonable. I just... I haven't really thought about exactly what it would take, because it's just been there every time I look. But I'm open to whatever you suggest. If you think there's a way to help me see it differently, I'll put in the work, 100 percent. \n\nIs that... I mean, is it normal to not really know what would change my mind? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of answers here. I just want to be as helpful as I can.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
